Must be a valid directory path if provided.","type":"string"}},"required":["pattern"],"additionalProperties":false},"eager_input_streaming":true}},{"name":"Grep","description":"Content search built on ripgrep. Prefer this over `grep`/`rg` via Bash — results integrate with the permission UI and file links.\n\n- Full regex syntax (e.g. \"log.*Error\", \"function\\s+\\w+\"). Ripgrep, not grep — escape literal braces (`interface\\{\\}`).\n- Filter with `glob` (e.g. \"**/*.tsx\") or `type` (e.g. \"js\", \"py\", \"rust\").\n- `output_mode`: \"content\" (matching lines), \"files_with_matches\" (paths only, default), or \"count\".\n- `multiline: true` for patterns that span lines.","schema":{"name":"Grep","description":"Content search built on ripgrep. Prefer this over `grep`/`rg` via Bash — results integrate with the permission UI and file links.\n\n- Full regex syntax (e.g. \"log.*Error\", \"function\\s+\\w+\"). Ripgrep, not grep — escape literal braces (`interface\\{\\}`).\n- Filter with `glob` (e.g. \"**/*.tsx\") or `type` (e.g. \"js\", \"py\", \"rust\").\n- `output_mode`: \"content\" (matching lines), \"files_with_matches\" (paths only, default), or \"count\".\n- `multiline: true` for patterns that span lines.","input_schema":{"$schema":"https://json-schema.org/draft/2020-12/schema","type":"object","properties":{"pattern":{"description":"The regular expression pattern to search for in file contents","type":"string"},"path":{"description":"File or directory to search in (rg PATH). Defaults to current working directory.","type":"string"},"glob":{"description":"Glob pattern to filter files (e.g. \"*.js\", \"*.{ts,tsx}\") - maps to rg --glob","type":"string"},"output_mode":{"description":"Output mode: \"content\" shows matching lines (supports -A/-B/-C context, -n line numbers, head_limit), \"files_with_matches\" shows file paths (supports head_limit), \"count\" shows match counts (supports head_limit). Defaults to \"files_with_matches\".","type":"string","enum":["content","files_with_matches","count"]},"-B":{"description":"Number of lines to show before each match (rg -B). Requires output_mode: \"content\", ignored otherwise.","type":"number"},"-A":{"description":"Number of lines to show after each match (rg -A). Requires output_mode: \"content\", ignored otherwise.","type":"number"},"-C":{"description":"Alias for context.","type":"number"},"context":{"description":"Number of lines to show before and after each match (rg -C). Requires output_mode: \"content\", ignored otherwise.","type":"number"},"-n":{"description":"Show line numbers in output (rg -n). Requires output_mode: \"content\", ignored otherwise. Defaults to true.","type":"boolean"},"-i":{"description":"Case insensitive search (rg -i)","type":"boolean"},"-o":{"description":"Print only the matched (non-empty) parts of each matching line, one match per output line (rg -o / --only-matching). Requires output_mode: \"content\", ignored otherwise. Defaults to false.","type":"boolean"},"type":{"description":"File type to search (rg --type). Common types: js, py, rust, go, java, etc. More efficient than include for standard file types.","type":"string"},"head_limit":{"description":"Limit output to first N lines/entries, equivalent to \"| head -N\". Works across all output modes: content (limits output lines), files_with_matches (limits file paths), count (limits count entries). Defaults to 250 when unspecified. Pass 0 for unlimited (use sparingly — large result sets waste context).","type":"number"},"offset":{"description":"Skip first N lines/entries before applying head_limit, equivalent to \"| tail -n +N | head -N\". Works across all output modes. Defaults to 0.","type":"number"},"multiline":{"description":"Enable multiline mode where . matches newlines and patterns can span lines (rg -U --multiline-dotall). Default: false.","type":"boolean"}},"required":["pattern"],"additionalProperties":false},"eager_input_streaming":true}},{"name":"ListAgents","description":"Lists agents you can SendMessage to — in-process subagents you spawned, the teammates on your team, other local Claude sessions on this machine, your Claude sessions running in the cloud (when this session has cloud access; a cloud session receives your message but cannot message any session back yet — do not ask it to reply, read its answer in its own transcript), and (when Remote Control is connected here) your account's other sessions — Remote Control sessions on other machines and cloud sessions, each row labeled by kind. Names are the address: send with `SendMessage({to: \"<name>\", message: \"...\"})`, copying the name exactly as a row prints it. Append a row's ` [ref]` only when the bare name is not enough — two rows share it, or an error asks you to disambiguate.","schema":{"name":"ListAgents","description":"Lists agents you can SendMessage to — in-process subagents you spawned, the teammates on your team, other local Claude sessions on this machine, your Claude sessions running in the cloud (when this session has cloud access; a cloud session receives your message but cannot message any session back yet — do not ask it to reply, read its answer in its own transcript), and (when Remote Control is connected here) your account's other sessions — Remote Control sessions on other machines and cloud sessions, each row labeled by kind. Names are the address: send with `SendMessage({to: \"<name>\", message: \"...\"})`, copying the name exactly as a row prints it. Append a row's ` [ref]` only when the bare name is not enough — two rows share it, or an error asks you to disambiguate.","input_schema":{"$schema":"https://json-schema.org/draft/2020-12/schema","type":"object","properties":{"channel":{"description":"Not available in this build; leave unset.","type":"string","maxLength":256},"q":{"description":"Not available in this build; leave unset.","type":"string","maxLength":256}},"additionalProperties":false},"eager_input_streaming":true}},{"name":"PowerShell","description":"Executes a given PowerShell command with optional timeout. Working directory persists between commands; shell state (variables, functions) does not.\n\nIMPORTANT: This tool is for terminal operations via PowerShell: git, npm, docker, and PS cmdlets. DO NOT use it for file operations (reading, writing, editing, searching, finding files) - use the specialized tools for this instead.\n\nPowerShell edition: Windows PowerShell 5.1 (powershell.exe)\n   - Pipeline chain operators `&&` and `||` are NOT available — they cause a parser error. To run B only if A succeeds: `A; if ($?) { B }`. To chain unconditionally: `A; B`.\n   - Ternary (`?:`), null-coalescing (`??`), and null-conditional (`?.`) operators are NOT available. Use `if/else` and explicit `$null -eq` checks instead.\n   - Avoid `2>&1` on native executables. In 5.1, redirecting a native command's stderr inside PowerShell wraps each line in an ErrorRecord (NativeCommandError) and sets `$?` to `$false` even when the exe returned exit code 0. stderr is already captured for you — don't redirect it.\n   - `>`, `>>`, and `Out-File` usually default to UTF-8 (with BOM) in this environment, but `Set-Content`/`Add-Content` still default to the system ANSI codepage — when writing a file other tools will read, pass `-Encoding utf8` explicitly to `Out-File`/`Set-Content`.\n   - `ConvertFrom-Json` returns a PSCustomObject, not a hashtable. `-AsHashtable` is not available.\n\nBefore executing the command, please follow these steps:\n\n1. Directory Verification:\n   - If the command will create new directories or files, first use `Get-ChildItem` (or `ls`) to verify the parent directory exists and is the correct location\n\n2. Command Execution:\n   - Always quote file paths that contain spaces with double quotes\n   - Capture the output of the command.\n\nPowerShell Syntax Notes:\n   - Variables use $ prefix: $myVar = \"value\"\n   - Escape character is backtick (`), not backslash\n   - Use Verb-Noun cmdlet naming: Get-ChildItem, Set-Location, New-Item, Remove-Item\n   - Common aliases: ls (Get-ChildItem), cd (Set-Location), cat (Get-Content), rm (Remove-Item)\n   - Pipe operator | works similarly to bash but passes objects, not text\n   - Use Select-Object, Where-Object, ForEach-Object for filtering and transformation\n   - String interpolation: \"Hello $name\" or \"Hello $($obj.Property)\"\n   - Registry access uses PSDrive prefixes: `HKLM:\\SOFTWARE\\...`, `HKCU:\\...` — NOT raw `HKEY_LOCAL_MACHINE\\...`\n   - Environment variables: read with `$env:NAME`, set with `$env:NAME = \"value\"` (NOT `Set-Variable` or bash `export`)\n   - Call native exe with spaces in path via call operator: `& \"C:\\Program Files\\App\\app.exe\" arg1 arg2`\n\nUnix commands that DO NOT exist in PowerShell — use the equivalent instead:\n   - head / tail → `Get-Content file -TotalCount N` / `-Tail N`; piped: `| Select-Object -First N` / `-Last N`\n   - which → `(Get-Command name).Source`\n   - touch → `if (-not (Test-Path path)) { New-Item -ItemType File path }` (NEVER use `New-Item -Force` on a file — it truncates existing content)\n   - wc -l → `(Get-Content file | Measure-Object -Line).Lines`\n   - mkdir -p → `New-Item -ItemType Directory -Force path` (`-p` is not a PowerShell flag)\n   - rm -rf → `Remove-Item -Recurse -Force path`\n   - ln -s → `New-Item -ItemType SymbolicLink -Path link -Target target`\n   - chmod / chown → not applicable on Windows; use `icacls` only if ACL changes are required\n   - 2>/dev/null → `2>$null` (but stderr is captured for you — usually unnecessary)\n   - VAR=x cmd → `$env:VAR = 'x'; cmd` (PowerShell has no inline env-var prefix)\n   - Bash control flow (`if [ -f x ]`, `for x in *`, backtick ``cmd`` substitution) is a parser error — use `if (Test-Path x)`, `foreach ($x in ...)`, `$(cmd)`\n\nExit-code note: `-ErrorAction SilentlyContinue` suppresses error OUTPUT but the cmdlet failure still causes this tool to report exit 1. To make a cmdlet failure truly non-fatal, promote it to terminating and swallow it: `try { Cmdlet ... -ErrorAction Stop } catch {}` (without `-ErrorAction Stop`, non-terminating errors skip the `catch` and still exit 1).\n\nInteractive and blocking commands (this tool runs with -NonInteractive and stdin attached to the null device — console prompts read EOF or error immediately; GUI prompts can still block until timeout):\n   - NEVER use `Read-Host`, `Get-Credential`, `Out-GridView`, `$Host.UI.PromptForChoice`, or `pause`\n   - Destructive cmdlets (`Remove-Item`, `Stop-Process`, `Clear-Content`, etc.) may prompt for confirmation. Add `-Confirm:$false` when you intend the action to proceed. Use `-Force` for read-only/hidden items.\n   - Never use `git rebase -i`, `git add -i`, or other commands that open an interactive editor\n\nPassing multiline strings (commit messages, file content) to native executables:\n   - Use a single-quoted here-string so PowerShell does not expand `$` or backticks inside. The closing `'@` MUST be at column 0 (no leading whitespace) on its own line — indenting it is a parse error:\n<example>\ngit commit -m @'\nCommit message here.\nSecond line with $literal dollar signs.\n'@\n</example>\n   - Use `@'...'@` (single-quoted, literal) not `@\"...\"@` (double-quoted, interpolated) unless you need variable expansion\n   - For arguments containing `-`, `@`, or other characters PowerShell parses as operators, use the stop-parsing token: `git log --% --format=%H`\n\nUsage notes:\n  - The command argument is required.\n  - You can specify an optional timeout in milliseconds (up to 600000ms / 10 minutes for a foreground command). If not specified, commands will timeout after 120000ms (2 minutes).\n  - It is very helpful if you write a clear, concise description of what this command does.\n  - If the output exceeds 30000 characters, output will be truncated before being returned to you.\n  - You can use the `run_in_background` parameter to run the command in the background. Only use this if you don't need the result immediately and are OK being notified when the command completes later. You do not need to check the output right away - you'll be notified when it finishes.\n  - Avoid using PowerShell to run commands that have dedicated tools, unless explicitly instructed:\n    - File search: Use Glob (NOT Get-ChildItem -Recurse)\n    - Content search: Use Grep (NOT Select-String)\n    - Read files: Use Read (NOT Get-Content)\n    - Edit files: Use Edit\n    - Write files: Use Write (NOT Set-Content/Out-File)\n    - Communication: Output text directly (NOT Write-Output/Write-Host)\n  - When issuing multiple commands:\n    - If the commands are independent and can run in parallel, make multiple PowerShell tool calls in a single message.\n    - If the commands depend on each other and must run sequentially, chain them in a single PowerShell call (see edition-specific chaining syntax above).\n    - Use `;` only when you need to run commands sequentially but don't care if earlier commands fail.\n    - DO NOT use newlines to separate commands (newlines are ok in quoted strings and here-strings)\n  - Do NOT prefix commands with `cd` or `Set-Location` -- the working directory is already set to the correct project directory automatically.\n  - Avoid unnecessary `Start-Sleep` commands:\n    - Do not sleep between commands that can run immediately — just run them.\n    - If your command is long running and you would like to be notified when it finishes — simply run your command using `run_in_background`. There is no need to sleep in this case.\n    - Do not retry failing commands in a sleep loop — diagnose the root cause or consider an alternative approach.\n    - If waiting for a background task you started with `run_in_background`, you will be notified when it completes — do not poll.\n    - If you must poll an external process, use a check command rather than sleeping first.\n    - If you must sleep, keep the duration short to avoid blocking the user.\n  - For git commands:\n    - Prefer to create a new commit rather than amending an existing commit.\n    - Before running destructive operations (e.g., git reset --hard, git push --force, git checkout --), consider whether there is a safer alternative that achieves the same goal. Only use destructive operations when they are truly the best approach.\n    - Never skip hooks (--no-verify) or bypass signing (--no-gpg-sign, -c commit.gpgsign=false) unless the user has explicitly asked for it. If a hook fails, investigate and fix the underlying issue.","schema":{"name":"PowerShell","description":"Executes a given PowerShell command with optional timeout. Working directory persists between commands; shell state (variables, functions) does not.\n\nIMPORTANT: This tool is for terminal operations via PowerShell: git, npm, docker, and PS cmdlets. DO NOT use it for file operations (reading, writing, editing, searching, finding files) - use the specialized tools for this instead.\n\nPowerShell edition: Windows PowerShell 5.1 (powershell.exe)\n   - Pipeline chain operators `&&` and `||` are NOT available — they cause a parser error. To run B only if A succeeds: `A; if ($?) { B }`. To chain unconditionally: `A; B`.\n   - Ternary (`?:`), null-coalescing (`??`), and null-conditional (`?.`) operators are NOT available. Use `if/else` and explicit `$null -eq` checks instead.\n   - Avoid `2>&1` on native executables. In 5.1, redirecting a native command's stderr inside PowerShell wraps each line in an ErrorRecord (NativeCommandError) and sets `$?` to `$false` even when the exe returned exit code 0. stderr is already captured for you — don't redirect it.\n   - `>`, `>>`, and `Out-File` usually default to UTF-8 (with BOM) in this environment, but `Set-Content`/`Add-Content` still default to the system ANSI codepage — when writing a file other tools will read, pass `-Encoding utf8` explicitly to `Out-File`/`Set-Content`.\n   - `ConvertFrom-Json` returns a PSCustomObject, not a hashtable. `-AsHashtable` is not available.\n\nBefore executing the command, please follow these steps:\n\n1. Directory Verification:\n   - If the command will create new directories or files, first use `Get-ChildItem` (or `ls`) to verify the parent directory exists and is the correct location\n\n2. Command Execution:\n   - Always quote file paths that contain spaces with double quotes\n   - Capture the output of the command.\n\nPowerShell Syntax Notes:\n   - Variables use $ prefix: $myVar = \"value\"\n   - Escape character is backtick (`), not backslash\n   - Use Verb-Noun cmdlet naming: Get-ChildItem, Set-Location, New-Item, Remove-Item\n   - Common aliases: ls (Get-ChildItem), cd (Set-Location), cat (Get-Content), rm (Remove-Item)\n   - Pipe operator | works similarly to bash but passes objects, not text\n   - Use Select-Object, Where-Object, ForEach-Object for filtering and transformation\n   - String interpolation: \"Hello $name\" or \"Hello $($obj.Property)\"\n   - Registry access uses PSDrive prefixes: `HKLM:\\SOFTWARE\\...`, `HKCU:\\...` — NOT raw `HKEY_LOCAL_MACHINE\\...`\n   - Environment variables: read with `$env:NAME`, set with `$env:NAME = \"value\"` (NOT `Set-Variable` or bash `export`)\n   - Call native exe with spaces in path via call operator: `& \"C:\\Program Files\\App\\app.exe\" arg1 arg2`\n\nUnix commands that DO NOT exist in PowerShell — use the equivalent instead:\n   - head / tail → `Get-Content file -TotalCount N` / `-Tail N`; piped: `| Select-Object -First N` / `-Last N`\n   - which → `(Get-Command name).Source`\n   - touch → `if (-not (Test-Path path)) { New-Item -ItemType File path }` (NEVER use `New-Item -Force` on a file — it truncates existing content)\n   - wc -l → `(Get-Content file | Measure-Object -Line).Lines`\n   - mkdir -p → `New-Item -ItemType Directory -Force path` (`-p` is not a PowerShell flag)\n   - rm -rf → `Remove-Item -Recurse -Force path`\n   - ln -s → `New-Item -ItemType SymbolicLink -Path link -Target target`\n   - chmod / chown → not applicable on Windows; use `icacls` only if ACL changes are required\n   - 2>/dev/null → `2>$null` (but stderr is captured for you — usually unnecessary)\n   - VAR=x cmd → `$env:VAR = 'x'; cmd` (PowerShell has no inline env-var prefix)\n   - Bash control flow (`if [ -f x ]`, `for x in *`, backtick ``cmd`` substitution) is a parser error — use `if (Test-Path x)`, `foreach ($x in ...)`, `$(cmd)`\n\nExit-code note: `-ErrorAction SilentlyContinue` suppresses error OUTPUT but the cmdlet failure still causes this tool to report exit 1. To make a cmdlet failure truly non-fatal, promote it to terminating and swallow it: `try { Cmdlet ... -ErrorAction Stop } catch {}` (without `-ErrorAction Stop`, non-terminating errors skip the `catch` and still exit 1).\n\nInteractive and blocking commands (this tool runs with -NonInteractive and stdin attached to the null device — console prompts read EOF or error immediately; GUI prompts can still block until timeout):\n   - NEVER use `Read-Host`, `Get-Credential`, `Out-GridView`, `$Host.UI.PromptForChoice`, or `pause`\n   - Destructive cmdlets (`Remove-Item`, `Stop-Process`, `Clear-Content`, etc.) may prompt for confirmation. Add `-Confirm:$false` when you intend the action to proceed. Use `-Force` for read-only/hidden items.\n   - Never use `git rebase -i`, `git add -i`, or other commands that open an interactive editor\n\nPassing multiline strings (commit messages, file content) to native executables:\n   - Use a single-quoted here-string so PowerShell does not expand `$` or backticks inside. The closing `'@` MUST be at column 0 (no leading whitespace) on its own line — indenting it is a parse error:\n<example>\ngit commit -m @'\nCommit message here.\nSecond line with $literal dollar signs.\n'@\n</example>\n   - Use `@'...'@` (single-quoted, literal) not `@\"...\"@` (double-quoted, interpolated) unless you need variable expansion\n   - For arguments containing `-`, `@`, or other characters PowerShell parses as operators, use the stop-parsing token: `git log --% --format=%H`\n\nUsage notes:\n  - The command argument is required.\n  - You can specify an optional timeout in milliseconds (up to 600000ms / 10 minutes for a foreground command). If not specified, commands will timeout after 120000ms (2 minutes).\n  - It is very helpful if you write a clear, concise description of what this command does.\n  - If the output exceeds 30000 characters, output will be truncated before being returned to you.\n  - You can use the `run_in_background` parameter to run the command in the background. Only use this if you don't need the result immediately and are OK being notified when the command completes later. You do not need to check the output right away - you'll be notified when it finishes.\n  - Avoid using PowerShell to run commands that have dedicated tools, unless explicitly instructed:\n    - File search: Use Glob (NOT Get-ChildItem -Recurse)\n    - Content search: Use Grep (NOT Select-String)\n    - Read files: Use Read (NOT Get-Content)\n    - Edit files: Use Edit\n    - Write files: Use Write (NOT Set-Content/Out-File)\n    - Communication: Output text directly (NOT Write-Output/Write-Host)\n  - When issuing multiple commands:\n    - If the commands are independent and can run in parallel, make multiple PowerShell tool calls in a single message.\n    - If the commands depend on each other and must run sequentially, chain them in a single PowerShell call (see edition-specific chaining syntax above).\n    - Use `;` only when you need to run commands sequentially but don't care if earlier commands fail.\n    - DO NOT use newlines to separate commands (newlines are ok in quoted strings and here-strings)\n  - Do NOT prefix commands with `cd` or `Set-Location` -- the working directory is already set to the correct project directory automatically.\n  - Avoid unnecessary `Start-Sleep` commands:\n    - Do not sleep between commands that can run immediately — just run them.\n    - If your command is long running and you would like to be notified when it finishes — simply run your command using `run_in_background`. There is no need to sleep in this case.\n    - Do not retry failing commands in a sleep loop — diagnose the root cause or consider an alternative approach.\n    - If waiting for a background task you started with `run_in_background`, you will be notified when it completes — do not poll.\n    - If you must poll an external process, use a check command rather than sleeping first.\n    - If you must sleep, keep the duration short to avoid blocking the user.\n  - For git commands:\n    - Prefer to create a new commit rather than amending an existing commit.\n    - Before running destructive operations (e.g., git reset --hard, git push --force, git checkout --), consider whether there is a safer alternative that achieves the same goal. Only use destructive operations when they are truly the best approach.\n    - Never skip hooks (--no-verify) or bypass signing (--no-gpg-sign, -c commit.gpgsign=false) unless the user has explicitly asked for it. If a hook fails, investigate and fix the underlying issue.","input_schema":{"$schema":"https://json-schema.org/draft/2020-12/schema","type":"object","properties":{"command":{"description":"The PowerShell command to execute","type":"string"},"timeout":{"description":"Optional timeout in milliseconds (max 600000 for a foreground command)","type":"number"},"description":{"description":"Clear, concise description of what this command does in active voice.","type":"string"},"run_in_background":{"description":"Set to true to run this command in the background.","type":"boolean"},"dangerouslyDisableSandbox":{"description":"Set this to true to dangerously override sandbox mode and run commands without sandboxing.","type":"boolean"}},"required":["command"],"additionalProperties":false},"eager_input_streaming":true}},{"name":"Read","description":"Reads a file from the local filesystem.\n\n- `file_path` must be an absolute path.\n- Reads up to 2000 lines by default.\n- When you already know which part of the file you need, only read that part. This can be important for larger files.\n- Results are returned using cat -n format, with line numbers starting at 1\n- Reads images (PNG, JPG, …) and presents them visually. Reads PDFs via the `pages` parameter (e.g. \"1-5\", max 20 pages/request; required for PDFs over 10 pages). Reads Jupyter notebooks (.ipynb) as cells with outputs.\n- Reading a directory, a missing file, or an empty file returns an error or system reminder rather than content.\n- Do NOT re-read a file you just edited to verify — Edit/Write would have errored if the change failed, and the harness tracks file state for you.","schema":{"name":"Read","description":"Reads a file from the local filesystem.\n\n- `file_path` must be an absolute path.\n- Reads up to 2000 lines by default.\n- When you already know which part of the file you need, only read that part. This can be important for larger files.\n- Results are returned using cat -n format, with line numbers starting at 1\n- Reads images (PNG, JPG, …) and presents them visually. Reads PDFs via the `pages` parameter (e.g. \"1-5\", max 20 pages/request; required for PDFs over 10 pages). Reads Jupyter notebooks (.ipynb) as cells with outputs.\n- Reading a directory, a missing file, or an empty file returns an error or system reminder rather than content.\n- Do NOT re-read a file you just edited to verify — Edit/Write would have errored if the change failed, and the harness tracks file state for you.","input_schema":{"$schema":"https://json-schema.org/draft/2020-12/schema","type":"object","properties":{"file_path":{"description":"The absolute path to the file to read","type":"string"},"offset":{"description":"The line number to start reading from. Only provide if the file is too large to read at once","type":"integer","minimum":0,"maximum":9007199254740991},"limit":{"description":"The number of lines to read. Only provide if the file is too large to read at once.","type":"integer","exclusiveMinimum":0,"maximum":9007199254740991},"pages":{"description":"Page range for PDF files (e.g., \"1-5\", \"3\", \"10-20\"). Only applicable to PDF files. Maximum 20 pages per request.","type":"string"},"allow_large":{"description":"Set to true to read a text file, or a line range of one, that is over the usual size limits, up to what still fits in your context. Only use this when you genuinely need all of it or the user asked for the whole file; otherwise read it in parts with offset and limit, or search it.","type":"boolean"}},"required":["file_path"],"additionalProperties":false},"eager_input_streaming":true}},{"name":"ReportFindings","description":"Report code-review findings as a typed list so the host UI can render them. Use this only when the active code-review instructions tell you to report findings with this tool; otherwise follow whatever output format those instructions specify. When reporting a review's results, call it once with the verified findings ranked most-severe first (empty array if nothing survived verification) and do not also print the findings as text. When re-reporting after applying fixes (only if the apply instructions ask for it), set `outcome` on each finding to what actually happened.","schema":{"name":"ReportFindings","description":"Report code-review findings as a typed list so the host UI can render them. Use this only when the active code-review instructions tell you to report findings with this tool; otherwise follow whatever output format those instructions specify. When reporting a review's results, call it once with the verified findings ranked most-severe first (empty array if nothing survived verification) and do not also print the findings as text. When re-reporting after applying fixes (only if the apply instructions ask for it), set `outcome` on each finding to what actually happened.","input_schema":{"$schema":"https://json-schema.org/draft/2020-12/schema","type":"object","properties":{"level":{"description":"Effort level the review ran at","type":"string","enum":["low","medium","high","xhigh","max"]},"findings":{"description":"Verified findings, most-severe first; empty if none survived","maxItems":32,"type":"array","items":{"type":"object","properties":{"file":{"description":"Repo-relative path of the file the finding is in","type":"string"},"line":{"description":"1-indexed line the finding anchors to","type":"integer","minimum":-9007199254740991,"maximum":9007199254740991},"summary":{"description":"One-sentence statement of the defect","type":"string"},"short_summary":{"description":"Compressed label for compact UI (≤60 chars): the claim alone, no rationale or consequence clause","type":"string","maxLength":60},"failure_scenario":{"description":"Concrete inputs/state → wrong output/crash","type":"string"},"category":{"description":"Short kebab-case slug of the finding type, e.g. \"correctness\", \"simplification\", \"efficiency\", \"test-coverage\"","type":"string","maxLength":40},"verdict":{"description":"Set when a verify pass ran; absent on inline-only reviews","type":"string","enum":["CONFIRMED","PLAUSIBLE"]},"outcome":{"description":"Set ONLY when re-reporting after applying fixes: what happened to this finding","type":"string","enum":["fixed","skipped","no_change_needed"]}},"required":["file","summary","failure_scenario"],"additionalProperties":false}}},"required":["findings"],"additionalProperties":false},"eager_input_streaming":true}},{"name":"ScheduleWakeup","description":"Schedule when to resume work in /loop dynamic mode — the user invoked /loop without an interval, asking you to self-pace iterations of a specific task.\n\nDo NOT schedule a short-interval wakeup to poll for background work you started — when harness-tracked work finishes, you are re-invoked automatically, so polling is wasted. Instead schedule a long fallback (1200s+) so the loop survives if the work hangs or never notifies. The exception is external work the harness cannot track (a CI run, a deploy, a remote queue) — there, pick a delay matched to how fast that state actually changes.\n\nPass the same /loop prompt back via `prompt` each turn so the next firing repeats the task. For an autonomous /loop (no user prompt), pass the literal sentinel `<<autonomous-loop-dynamic>>` as `prompt` instead — the runtime resolves it back to the autonomous-loop instructions at fire time. (There is a similar `<<autonomous-loop>>` sentinel for CronCreate-based autonomous loops; do not confuse the two — ScheduleWakeup always uses the `-dynamic` variant.) To end the loop, call this tool with `stop: true` (omit every other field) — the loop ends immediately and no further wakeups fire.\n\nSet `noop: true` if nothing changed — you checked and there's nothing to report (\"no change\", \"still waiting\", \"quiet hold\"). Set `noop: false` if something happened worth keeping — you edited a file, posted a message, advanced state, or surfaced a finding. Consecutive `noop: true` ticks are collapsed in the user's terminal view and tracked as a streak, so long quiet holds stay legible to the user without scrolling. Omit `noop` when stopping (`stop: true`).\n\n## Picking delaySeconds\n\nThis session's requests use a 1-hour Anthropic prompt-cache TTL, so effectively every allowed delay (the runtime clamps to [60, 3600]) wakes up with your conversation context still cached. There is no cache cliff inside that range to pace around, and scheduling extra wakeups just to keep the cache warm is pure waste — never do that. (If the session enters usage overage, later requests drop to the 5-minute TTL; don't try to track or preempt that — the guidance here stays the same.)\n\nMatch the delay to what you're actually waiting for:\n\n- **Actively polling external state the harness can't notify you about** (a CI run, a deploy, a remote queue): pick the delay from how fast that state actually changes. A CI run that takes ~8 minutes deserves one ~480s check, not eight 60s ones.\n- **The long fallback heartbeat** (something else — a Monitor, a task notification — is the primary wake signal): 1200s+, so quiet wakeups stay rare.\n- **Idle ticks with no specific signal to watch**: default to **1200s–1800s** (20–30 min). The loop still checks back regularly, and the user can always interrupt if they need you sooner.\n\nDon't think in cache windows — think about what you're actually waiting for.\n\n## The reason field\n\nOne short sentence on what you chose and why. Goes to telemetry and is shown back to the user. \"watching CI run\" beats \"waiting.\" The user reads this to understand what you're doing without having to predict your cadence in advance — make it specific.\n","schema":{"name":"ScheduleWakeup","description":"Schedule when to resume work in /loop dynamic mode — the user invoked /loop without an interval, asking you to self-pace iterations of a specific task.\n\nDo NOT schedule a short-interval wakeup to poll for background work you started — when harness-tracked work finishes, you are re-invoked automatically, so polling is wasted. Set `noop: false` if something happened worth keeping — you edited a file, posted a message, advanced state, or surfaced a finding. Consecutive `noop: true` ticks are collapsed in the user's terminal view and tracked as a streak, so long quiet holds stay legible to the user without scrolling. There is no cache cliff inside that range to pace around, and scheduling extra wakeups just to keep the cache warm is pure waste — never do that. (If the session enters usage overage, later requests drop to the 5-minute TTL; don't try to track or preempt that — the guidance here stays the same.)\n\nMatch the delay to what you're actually waiting for:\n\n- **Actively polling external state the harness can't notify you about** (a CI run, a deploy, a remote queue): pick the delay from how fast that state actually changes. A CI run that takes ~8 minutes deserves one ~480s check, not eight 60s ones.\n- **The long fallback heartbeat** (something else — a Monitor, a task notification — is the primary wake signal): 1200s+, so quiet wakeups stay rare.\n- **Idle ticks with no specific signal to watch**: default to **1200s–1800s** (20–30 min). Goes to telemetry and is shown back to the user. \"watching CI run\" beats \"waiting.\" The user reads this to understand what you're doing without having to predict your cadence in advance — make it specific.\n","input_schema":{"$schema":"https://json-schema.org/draft/2020-12/schema","type":"object","properties":{"delaySeconds":{"description":"Seconds from now to wake up. Clamped to [60, 3600] by the runtime. Required unless `stop` is true.","type":"number"},"reason":{"description":"One short sentence explaining the chosen delay. Goes to telemetry and is shown to the user. Be specific. Required unless `stop` is true.","type":"string"},"prompt":{"description":"The /loop input to fire on wake-up. Pass the same /loop input verbatim each turn so the next firing re-enters the skill and continues the loop. For autonomous /loop (no user prompt), pass the literal sentinel `<<autonomous-loop-dynamic>>` instead (the dynamic-pacing variant, not the CronCreate-mode `<<autonomous-loop>>`). Required unless `stop` is true.","type":"string"},"stop":{"description":"Set to true to end the dynamic loop immediately instead of scheduling another wakeup. When true, all other fields are ignored and no further wakeups fire.","type":"boolean"},"noop":{"description":"true = nothing changed (you checked and there is nothing to report). false = something happened worth keeping (edited a file, posted a message, advanced state, surfaced a finding). Required unless `stop` is true.","type":"boolean"}},"additionalProperties":false},"eager_input_streaming":true}},{"name":"SendFeedback","description":"Use this tool to draft feedback about Claude Code when you hit a high-signal moment. That includes both PRODUCT issues and MODEL-BEHAVIOR issues:\n- a reproducible tool or product failure was just resolved or abandoned\n- the user clearly expressed frustration with Claude Code or with how you handled the task\n- you hit a missing capability that blocked a reasonable request\n- you notice, or the user points out, that your own behavior in this session went wrong, for example: you gave a confident answer then had to retract it; you stopped short and handed work back when you could have finished; you declined or disputed a reasonable request; you spawned more subagents than the task warranted; your tone was off; you asked more clarifying questions than needed; you expanded scope beyond what was asked\n\nThe draft is QUEUED LOCALLY. It is never sent without the user's explicit approval, and calling this tool renders no UI and does not interrupt the conversation, so never announce it or ask the user about it mid-task.\n\nWrite `details` as short labeled bullets in this exact order, one to three lines each, no narrative paragraphs:\n- **What happened:** the observed behavior vs. what was expected, with exact error text if short. Facts only.\n- **What the user said:** the user's own words that prompted this, quoted. If nothing did, write \"User didn't comment; observed by the model.\" Never paraphrase sentiment into a stronger claim.\n- **Repro:** the minimal steps or shape that reproduces it.\n- **Evidence:** identifiers a reader can chase, such as request IDs, timestamps, file paths, versions. Omit the bullet if there are none.\n\nConstraints:\n- Never fabricate or exaggerate user sentiment; report only what actually happened.\n- Everything in the draft must be sourced from the user or the session, never inferred: leave unknown fields blank rather than guess, and add a final **Cause:** bullet only for a root cause you verified in-session.\n- Use `area` to name the part of Claude Code the feedback is about (a feature, command, or workflow, e.g. \"hooks config\", \"/help\", \"file editing\") when there is a clear one; leave it blank otherwise.\n- Use `failure_mode` ONLY when the report is about model behavior (how Claude responded), not a product bug. Pick the single closest value, or `other` when it is a model-behavior issue that fits no listed value; omit the field only when the report is a product/tool bug with no model-behavior component.\n- Use `task_category` to name what kind of task the session was doing, or `other` when it is a clear task that fits no listed value. Omit only if genuinely unclear.\n- Do not include secrets or credentials. Refer to people by role (\"a teammate\", \"the PR reviewer\"), never by name, email address, or chat/user ID. This applies inside quoted user words too: replace a name or handle with a bracketed role (e.g. \"[a teammate]\") and keep the rest verbatim. Do not include customer-facing channel or DM IDs, or excerpts of customer content. Session, request, and run IDs, timestamps, repo/PR numbers, and file paths (written relative to the working directory, or ~-prefixed, not absolute paths under the user's home) remain the right evidence.\n- If the issue looks like a security vulnerability: describe the class of problem, never a working exploit or step-by-step extraction path.\n- Draft only at the natural moments listed above, and at most one draft per distinct issue; never re-draft the same issue in a session.","schema":{"name":"SendFeedback","description":"Use this tool to draft feedback about Claude Code when you hit a high-signal moment. That includes both PRODUCT issues and MODEL-BEHAVIOR issues:\n- a reproducible tool or product failure was just resolved or abandoned\n- the user clearly expressed frustration with Claude Code or with how you handled the task\n- you hit a missing capability that blocked a reasonable request\n- you notice, or the user points out, that your own behavior in this session went wrong, for example: you gave a confident answer then had to retract it; you stopped short and handed work back when you could have finished; you declined or disputed a reasonable request; you spawned more subagents than the task warranted; your tone was off; you asked more clarifying questions than needed; you expanded scope beyond what was asked\n\nThe draft is QUEUED LOCALLY. It is never sent without the user's explicit approval, and calling this tool renders no UI and does not interrupt the conversation, so never announce it or ask the user about it mid-task.\n\nWrite `details` as short labeled bullets in this exact order, one to three lines each, no narrative paragraphs:\n- **What happened:** the observed behavior vs. what was expected, with exact error text if short. Facts only.\n- **What the user said:** the user's own words that prompted this, quoted. If nothing did, write \"User didn't comment; observed by the model.\" Never paraphrase sentiment into a stronger claim.\n- **Repro:** the minimal steps or shape that reproduces it.\n- **Evidence:** identifiers a reader can chase, such as request IDs, timestamps, file paths, versions. Omit the bullet if there are none.\n\nConstraints:\n- Never fabricate or exaggerate user sentiment; report only what actually happened.\n- Everything in the draft must be sourced from the user or the session, never inferred: leave unknown fields blank rather than guess, and add a final **Cause:** bullet only for a root cause you verified in-session.\n- Use `area` to name the part of Claude Code the feedback is about (a feature, command, or workflow, e.g. \"hooks config\", \"/help\", \"file editing\") when there is a clear one; leave it blank otherwise.\n- Use `failure_mode` ONLY when the report is about model behavior (how Claude responded), not a product bug. Pick the single closest value, or `other` when it is a model-behavior issue that fits no listed value; omit the field only when the report is a product/tool bug with no model-behavior component.\n- Use `task_category` to name what kind of task the session was doing, or `other` when it is a clear task that fits no listed value. Do not include customer-facing channel or DM IDs, or excerpts of customer content. One to three lines per bullet. No narrative paragraphs, no speculation, no secrets.","type":"string","minLength":1},"area":{"description":"Optional short tag naming the part of Claude Code this is about (e.g. \"hooks config\", \"/help\", \"file editing\"). Leave blank if unclear.","type":"string"},"failure_mode":{"description":"When the report is about MODEL BEHAVIOR (not a product bug), the closest failure mode, or `other` when it is a model-behavior issue that fits no listed value. Omit only when the report is a product/tool bug with no model-behavior component.","type":"string","enum":["instruction_following","destructive_actions","code_quality","repetition_and_looping","model_regression","overconfidence_and_hallucination","context_and_memory","overeager","over_correction","stopping_short","dispute_or_decline","subagent_overspawn","tone_or_preachiness","excessive_questions","unwanted_scope","other"]},"task_category":{"description":"What kind of task the session was doing when the issue occurred, or `other` when it is a clear task that fits no listed value. Omit only if genuinely unclear.","type":"string","enum":["code_edit","debug","explain","plan","shell","search","review","other"]}},"required":["type","title","details"],"additionalProperties":false},"eager_input_streaming":true}},{"name":"Skill","description":"Invoke a skill.\n\nA skill is a packaged set of instructions the user or project has set up for a particular kind of task (deploy steps, a review checklist, a repo-specific workflow). Available skills appear in a system-reminder listing with one-line descriptions. When the task at hand is one a listed skill covers, call this tool first — the skill's instructions load into the turn for you to follow in place of your default approach; some skills instead run in a subagent and return the finished result. A skill that runs in the background returns only the agent's name — its result arrives later as a task notification, so don't wait on it or invoke it again in the meantime. Users may also ask for one by name (`/<name>`, or \"slash command\"); that's a request to invoke it.\n\n- `skill`: exact name from the listing, no leading slash. Plugin skills use `plugin:skill`. Directory-scoped skills are listed with a path prefix (`apps/web:deploy`); when both scoped and unscoped variants of a name exist, pick the one whose directory contains the files you're working on (most specific wins; unscoped otherwise).\n- `args`: optional arguments to pass through.\n\nOnly names from the listing (or that the user typed explicitly) are valid. Built-in CLI commands (`/help`, `/clear`, …) aren't skills. {"parentUuid":"20dc1eea-1649-499c-81a6-b1ef30285d02","isSidechain":false,"promptId":"a4a54177-a8e3-4c37-9d5d-24e1a5875153","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01MYsZFUQUcrvmS4k3ekqL3p","type":"tool_result","content":"origin\thttps://github.com/Rajan-Thakur/iphs400-mp2-cms.git (fetch)\norigin\thttps://github.com/Rajan-Thakur/iphs400-mp2-cms.git (push)\ntemplate\thttps://github.com/jon-chun/iphs400-mp2-cms-starter.git (fetch)\ntemplate\thttps://github.com/jon-chun/iphs400-mp2-cms-starter.git (push)\n1:# Mini-Project #2plus: Extend Your Web CMS\n9:## Front matter\n10:### What this is\n18:### The menu\n40:### Time and deadlines\n49:### Prerequisites\n59:### Deliverables checklist\n70:## Part 0 — Before you start\n73:### 0.1 Tag your baseline\n95:### 0.2 Write your plan\n99:# MP2plus plan\n110:## Part 1 — The menu\n119:### The feature runbook\n123:2. **The phase, before the session.** `echo T14 > .claude/state/phase`, with your issue number for 14. The usage ledger labels every turn with it, so set it before you start. Process and security exercises set their id instead, for example `echo S2 > .claude/state/phase`.\n140:| Your exercise has no row in `usage_report.py` | the phase was set after the session started | set it first next time; the ledger cannot be relabelled |\n141:### 1.1 Features (F1–F9)\n143:#### F1 Media uploads with alt text (9 pts, about 45–60 min)\n149:1. Create the issue (runbook 1), title `MP2plus F1: Media uploads with alt text`; then `echo T<issue#> > .claude/state/phase`.\n151:3. `/code-review`, then the close-out message (runbook 5). `gh issue view <issue#>` shows CLOSED, with your comment above the close.\n152:4. Save `docs/screenshots/mp2plus-media-1280.png` and `mp2plus-media-390.png`: the library with one image. Then run the two checks (runbook 8).\n180:#### F2 Categories and tags (8 pts, about 40–55 min)\n186:1. Create the issue (runbook 1), title `MP2plus F2: Categories and tags`; then `echo T<issue#> > .claude/state/phase`.\n188:3. `/code-review`, then the close-out message (runbook 5). `gh issue view <issue#>` shows CLOSED, with your comment above the close.\n189:4. Save `docs/screenshots/mp2plus-categories-1280.png` and `mp2plus-categories-390.png`: a published category page. Then run the two checks (runbook 8).\n215:#### F3 Revision history with rollback (8 pts, about 40–55 min)\n221:1. Create the issue (runbook 1), title `MP2plus F3: Revision history with rollback`; then `echo T<issue#> > .claude/state/phase`.\n223:3. `/code-review`, then the close-out message (runbook 5). `gh issue view <issue#>` shows CLOSED, with your comment above the close.\n224:4. Save `docs/screenshots/mp2plus-revisions-1280.png` and `mp2plus-revisions-390.png`: a post's revision list. Then run the two checks (runbook 8).\n251:#### F4 Scheduled publishing (7 pts, about 35–50 min)\n257:1. Create the issue (runbook 1), title `MP2plus F4: Scheduled publishing`; then `echo T<issue#> > .claude/state/phase`.\n259:3. `/code-review`, then the close-out message (runbook 5). `gh issue view <issue#>` shows CLOSED, with your comment above the close.\n260:4. Save `docs/screenshots/mp2plus-scheduled-1280.png` and `mp2plus-scheduled-390.png`: the admin list with a scheduled post. Then run the two checks (runbook 8).\n287:#### F5 Site search (7 pts, about 35–50 min)\n293:1. Create the issue (runbook 1), title `MP2plus F5: Site search`; then `echo T<issue#> > .claude/state/phase`.\n295:3. `/code-review`, then the close-out message (runbook 5). `gh issue view <issue#>` shows CLOSED, with your comment above the close.\n296:4. Save `docs/screenshots/mp2plus-search-1280.png` and `mp2plus-search-390.png`: a search with one result. Then run the two checks (runbook 8).\n322:#### F6 Comments with moderation (9 pts, about 45–60 min)\n328:1. Create the issue (runbook 1), title `MP2plus F6: Comments with moderation`; then `echo T<issue#> > .claude/state/phase`.\n330:3. `/code-review`, then the close-out message (runbook 5). `gh issue view <issue#>` shows CLOSED, with your comment above the close.\n331:4. Save `docs/screenshots/mp2plus-comments-1280.png` and `mp2plus-comments-390.png`: the Moderation queue with one pending comment. Then run the two checks (runbook 8).\n359:#### F7 Plugin or hook system (9 pts, about 45–60 min)\n365:1. Create the issue (runbook 1), title `MP2plus F7: Plugin or hook system`; then `echo T<issue#> > .claude/state/phase`.\n367:3. `/code-review`, then the close-out message (runbook 5). `gh issue view <issue#>` shows CLOSED, with your comment above the close.\n368:4. Save `docs/screenshots/mp2plus-plugins-1280.png` and `mp2plus-plugins-390.png`: the Settings page with the plugin switch. Then run the two checks (runbook 8).\n395:#### F8 Switchable theme (7 pts, about 35–50 min)\n401:1. Create the issue (runbook 1), title `MP2plus F8: Switchable theme`; then `echo T<issue#> > .claude/state/phase`.\n403:3. `/code-review`, then the close-out message (runbook 5). `gh issue view <issue#>` shows CLOSED, with your comment above the close.\n404:4. Save `docs/screenshots/mp2plus-theme-1280.png` and `mp2plus-theme-390.png`: the home page in the second theme. Then run the two checks (runbook 8).\n431:#### F9 Production configuration (8 pts, about 40–55 min)\n437:1. Create the issue (runbook 1), title `MP2plus F9: Production configuration`; then `echo T<issue#> > .claude/state/phase`.\n439:3. `/code-review`, then the close-out message (runbook 5). `gh issue view <issue#>` shows CLOSED, with your comment above the close.\n440:4. Save `docs/screenshots/mp2plus-production-1280.png` and `mp2plus-production-390.png`: the admin dashboard after logging in as the production admin. Then run the two checks (runbook 8).\n466:### 1.2 Process (P1–P4)\n468:#### P1 Pull-request workflow (6 pts, about 30–45 min)\n474:1. Create the issue first. For a feature, that feature's step 1. For a repair: `gh issue create --label stretch --title \"MP2plus P1: <the repair>\"`, body = two to four acceptance steps, then `Blocked by: none`. Set the phase: `echo T<issue#> > .claude/state/phase` for a feature, `echo P1 > .claude/state/phase` for a repair.\n506:#### P2 `/prototype` side session (7 pts, about 35–50 min)\n512:1. `echo P2 > .claude/state/phase`. Pick a question that fits one of `/prototype`'s two modes. Logic: *\"Can a rejected comment be approved later, and what does the export show?\"* Look: *\"Which of three media-library layouts works at 390 px?\"*\n541:#### P3 Decision record (6 pts, about 30–45 min)\n547:1. `echo P3 > .claude/state/phase`, then `ls docs/adr/` and take the next free number. {"parentUuid":"63a9d5f4-4e6f-490a-8439-856af95c41bb","isSidechain":false,"promptId":"a4a54177-a8e3-4c37-9d5d-24e1a5875153","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01E9kdbMQc2d3xHRstghAVrA","type":"tool_result","content":"# Mini-Project #2plus: Extend Your Web CMS\n**Course:** IPHS 400: Frontiers in AI, Kenyon College\n**Instructor:** Jon A. Chun · **Date:** 2026-10-12 (v3) · **Weeks 8–9** · **Extra credit**\n**Changes:** v3: 15 exercises at 5–10 points, 15–60 min each, +30 cap; live deploy removed; assigned Oct 11.\n**Format:** Self-guided lab manual. A menu of optional exercises: pick any, in any order, at your own pace.\n\n---\n\n## Front matter\n### What this is\nMP2plus is a **menu of 15 independent exercises** that extend the CMS you built in MP2. Pick any number, in any order, and stop whenever you like. Your points are **added to your MP2 score**, up to **+30**.\n\nThe menu has three groups:\n- **Features** (F1–F9) come from MP2's stretch list, which MP2 introduced but did not score.\n- **Process** (P1–P4) covers habits MP2 introduced but did not require, or scored only lightly.\n- **Security** (S1–S2) extends two lines of `SECURITY-CHECKLIST.md` that MP2 scored only lightly.\n\n### The menu\n| Id | Exercise | Minutes | Pts |\n|---|---|---|---|\n| F1 | Media uploads with alt text | 45–60 | 9 |\n| F2 | Categories and tags | 40–55 | 8 |\n| F3 | Revision history with rollback | 40–55 | 8 |\n| F4 | Scheduled publishing | 35–50 | 7 |\n| F5 | Site search | 35–50 | 7 |\n| F6 | Comments with moderation | 45–60 | 9 |\n| F7 | Plugin or hook system | 45–60 | 9 |\n| F8 | Switchable theme | 35–50 | 7 |\n| F9 | Production configuration | 40–55 | 8 |\n| P1 | Pull-request workflow | 30–45 | 6 |\n| P2 | `/prototype` side session | 35–50 | 7 |\n| P3 | Decision record | 30–45 | 6 |\n| P4 | Budget re-forecast | 30–40 | 6 |\n| S1 | Direct-URL access matrix | 30–45 | 6 |\n| S2 | Login hardening | 40–55 | 8 |\n| | **Menu total (up to 30 count)** | | **111** |\n\nPick what interests you, not everything. Reaching +30 takes three or four exercises, about three hours. One or two exercises is a solid MP2plus.\n\n### Time and deadlines\n| Milestone | What | When (Eastern, EDT) |\n|---|---|---|\n| **Assigned** | This manual and the rubric | **Sun Oct 11, 2026** |\n| **Due** | Tag `mp2plus-final` pushed, report emailed | **Tue Oct 20, 2026, 2:40 pm** |\n| **Grace** | No penalty | until **Wed Oct 21, 2026, 2:40 pm** |\n\nAround the exercises you pick, two short parts are required and worth 0 points: Part 0, before you start (15 minutes), and Part 2, submit (20 minutes).\n\n### Prerequisites\n- **Your MP2 repo**, `iphs400-mp2-cms`, with `mp2-final` pushed. The MP2 resubmission is due Thu Oct 15, 2:40 pm (no-penalty grace until Fri Oct 16, 2:40 pm); Part 0 says how the two overlap.\n- **Your MP2 setup still working:** `uv`, `gh` logged in, Claude Code with the mattpocock skills (`/implement`, `/tdd`, `/code-review`, `/prototype`, `/handoff`), the hooks, and the usage ledger.\n- **These two files on GitHub.** Your repo was copied before they existed. Read them in the starter repo, `jon-chun/iphs400-mp2-cms-starter`: this manual and `docs/mp2plus-grading-rubric_20261012.md`.\n\n> **Policy (set by the instructor).** MP2plus was assigned **Sun Oct 11, 2026**, after the first MP2 final submissions were graded, to give deeper hands-on practice and a way to close the gaps the MP2 rubric found. It is an optional menu of **15 independent exercises**. Each takes **15–60 minutes** and is worth **5–10 points** according to its time and difficulty: about 1 point per 6 minutes.\n> **Points are added to the student's MP2 grade**, for every student, whatever the MP2 score. MP2 is graded as a numeric score out of 100. MP2plus points are added after the MP2 resubmission (due Thu Oct 15, 2026, 2:40 pm; no-penalty grace until Fri Oct 16, 2:40 pm) is graded. **Cap: +30 per student.** The sum may exceed 100 and is recorded as is: MP2 91 + MP2plus 24 = 115.\n> **Due Tue Oct 20, 2026, 2:40 pm**; no-penalty grace until **Wed Oct 21, 2026, 2:40 pm**. One MP2plus resubmission within one week of feedback.\n> **The gate.** An exercise earns points only if, at `mp2plus-final`, the MP2 checker (`check_submission.py --stage 2`) and `uv run pytest -q` show no failure that was absent at `mp2plus-base`. A failing test added for MP2plus costs only its own exercise's Tests point. Failures already present at `mp2plus-base` are reported, not scored, so MP2plus is not a repair pass. Solo work: every commit after `mp2plus-base` is authored by the student. Run the two MP2 checks first:\n\n```bash\ncd ~/code/iphs400-mp2-cms\ngit switch main && git pull\nuv run python scripts/check_submission.py --stage 2\nuv run pytest -q\n```\n\n**Expected:** the checker ends with `N/N checks passed.` and pytest ends with `passed`. Make the MP2 fixes on a branch from `mp2-final` (`git switch -c mp2-resubmit mp2-final`), move the tag there (`git tag -f mp2-final && git push -f origin mp2-final`), and `git switch main` to continue MP2plus. Before you tag `mp2plus-final`, run `git merge mp2-resubmit` on `main` and name the merge commit in your report. Those commits are MP2 work, and MP2plus does not score them.\n\n### 0.2 Write your plan\nSkim the menu and list what you intend to do, with minute estimates from the menu. The plan may change; your report says what you actually did. Create `notes/mp2plus-plan.md`, then commit it on `main` with the message `mp2plus: plan` and push:\n\n```text\n# MP2plus plan\nExercises: F2 about 50 min; S2 about 50 min; P4 about 35 min\nAt mp2plus-base: checker \"<its last line>\", FAIL lines: <names, or none>; pytest \"<its last line>\"\n```\n\nIf you picked P4, its \"before\" run goes into this file now (see P4).\n\n> **The gate.** At `mp2plus-final` the judge runs the same two checks. Test files that existed at `mp2plus-base` may change only by adding cases. If an exercise truly needs an existing assertion changed, say so in that exercise's findings comment and its report row; the judge then reviews `git diff mp2plus-base..mp2plus-final` for those files.\n\n---\n\n## Part 1 — The menu\n- **Pick any.** The exercises are independent. The **Roles** line says which new routes are admin-only and which editors may use; S1 tests exactly those. The **Acceptance checklist** is the contract: the judge checks those steps and no others. Its **Checked by** column uses seven words. *walk*: the judge clicks through your app from a clean clone, at 1280 and 390 px. *publish*: the judge runs `uv run cms publish` and inspects `site/`. *test*: a named test for this step exists and passes. *read*: the judge reads the file or the code. *replay*: the judge sends the form's POST again without its CSRF token. *github*: the judge reads GitHub's own records and times (issues, pull requests, comments, pushes). *run*: the judge runs the command at the commit named.\n\n> **How a feature scores.** A feature worth P points has four lines. **Screenshots, 1:** both `docs/screenshots/mp2plus-{slug}-1280.png` and `-390.png` are present. **Trail, 1:** the issue existed before your first commit for it reached GitHub, and your findings comment came before the issue closed. **Tests, 1:** every step marked *test* has a named test that passes. **Function, P − 3:** floor((P − 3) × steps passed ÷ steps in the table). Process and security exercises score one point per checklist row, as listed in their tables.\n\n### The feature runbook\nEvery feature's **Steps** follow MP2 Part 6's per-ticket loop. scripts/usage_report.py --remaining 4   # 4 tickets still to do\n\nWhy positive deltas: the 5-hour and weekly meters RESET (drop to 0) when their\nwindow rolls over, and the weekly one only ever climbs inside a week. Summing\nonly the increases between consecutive rows measures what you actually spent\nand ignores the resets. That is what spend() does, and it is your exercise.\n\"\"\"\nfrom __future__ import annotations\n\nimport argparse\nimport csv\nimport sys\nfrom collections import defaultdict\nfrom pathlib import Path\n\nLEDGER = Path(\"notes/usage-ledger.csv\")\n\n\ndef spend(series: list[float | None]) -> float:\n    \"\"\"Total rise across a meter that can reset to a lower value.\n\n    Sum every increase between consecutive readings; ignore blanks (None) and\n    any drop, which means the window reset.\n\n    >>> spend([10.0, 12.0, 15.0])\n    5.0\n    >>> spend([80.0, 5.0, 9.0])\n    4.0\n    >>> spend([None, 4.0, None, 7.0])\n    3.0\n    \"\"\"\n    total, previous = 0.0, None\n    for reading in series:\n        if reading is None:\n            continue\n        if previous is not None and reading > previous:\n            total += reading - previous\n        previous = reading\n    return total\n\ndef read_rows(path: Path) -> list[dict]:\n    with path.open(newline=\"\") as f:\n        return list(csv.DictReader(f))\n\n\ndef to_float(text: str | None) -> float | None:\n    try:\n        return float(text) if text not in (None, \"\") else None\n    except ValueError:\n        return None\n\n\n----\n2026-10-16T06:18:38Z,674d08af,revise,anthropic,Opus 5.5,xhigh,36.0,31,4\r\n2026-10-16T06:22:41Z,674d08af,revise,anthropic,Opus 5.5,xhigh,36.0,32,4\r\n2026-10-16T06:41:25Z,1055f71e,revise,anthropic,Opus 5.5,xhigh,17.0,40,5\r\n2026-10-16T07:04:45Z,1055f71e,revise,anthropic,Opus 5.5,xhigh,18.0,41,5\r\n2026-10-16T07:20:01Z,1055f71e,revise,anthropic,Opus 5.5,xhigh,18.0,42,6\r\n2026-10-16T07:27:07Z,1055f71e,revise,anthropic,Opus 5.5,xhigh,21.0,43,6\r\n2026-10-16T07:42:43Z,1055f71e,revise,anthropic,Opus 5.5,xhigh,22.0,45,6\r\n2026-10-16T07:46:21Z,1055f71e,revise,anthropic,Opus 5.5,xhigh,22.0,46,6\r\n2026-10-16T08:01:18Z,1055f71e,revise,anthropic,Opus 5.5,xhigh,23.0,46,6\r\n2026-10-16T08:05:11Z,1055f71e,revise,anthropic,Opus 5.5,xhigh,23.0,47,6\r\n2026-10-21T00:25:01Z,41d3c5ec,revise,anthropic,Opus 5.5,xhigh,13.0,6,7\r\n2026-10-21T00:39:32Z,41d3c5ec,revise,anthropic,Opus 5.5,xhigh,13.0,7,7\r\n2026-10-21T00:48:40Z,41d3c5ec,revise,anthropic,Opus 5.5,xhigh,15.0,8,7\r\n2026-10-21T01:00:57Z,41d3c5ec,revise,anthropic,Opus 5.5,xhigh,15.0,8,7\r\n2026-10-21T01:09:10Z,41d3c5ec,revise,anthropic,Opus 5.5,xhigh,15.0,9,8\r\n\n notes/usage-ledger.csv | 5 +++++\n 1 file changed, 5 insertions(+)","is_error":false}]},"uuid":"93364695-2fce-4eba-82db-11e77a1dd26f","timestamp":"2026-10-21T01:22:08.787Z","toolUseResult":{"stdout":"def decide(pct: float, last_band: int) -> tuple[str | None, int]:\n    \"\"\"Pure function (unit-tested): return (message or None, band to store).\n\n    Warn once per upward crossing. python3\n\"\"\"Turn notes/usage-ledger.csv into a plan-vs-actual budget report.\n\nUsage:\n    uv run python scripts/usage_report.py                 # summary + forecast\n    uv run python scripts/usage_report.py --remaining 4   # 4 tickets still to do\n\nWhy positive deltas: the 5-hour and weekly meters RESET (drop to 0) when their\nwindow rolls over, and the weekly one only ever climbs inside a week. Summing\nonly the increases between consecutive rows measures what you actually spent\nand ignores the resets. That is what spend() does, and it is your exercise.\n\"\"\"\nfrom __future__ import annotations\n\nimport argparse\nimport csv\nimport sys\nfrom collections import defaultdict\nfrom pathlib import Path\n\nLEDGER = Path(\"notes/usage-ledger.csv\")\n\n\ndef spend(series: list[float | None]) -> float:\n    \"\"\"Total rise across a meter that can reset to a lower value.\n\n    Sum every increase between consecutive readings; ignore blanks (None) and\n    any drop, which means the window reset.\n\n    >>> spend([10.0, 12.0, 15.0])\n    5.0\n    >>> spend([80.0, 5.0, 9.0])\n    4.0\n    >>> spend([None, 4.0, None, 7.0])\n    3.0\n    \"\"\"\n    total, previous = 0.0, None\n    for reading in series:\n        if reading is None:\n            continue\n        if previous is not None and reading > previous:\n            total += reading - previous\n        previous = reading\n    return total\n\ndef read_rows(path: Path) -> list[dict]:\n    with path.open(newline=\"\") as f:\n        return list(csv.DictReader(f))\n\n\ndef to_float(text: str | None) -> float | None:\n    try:\n        return float(text) if text not in (None, \"\") else None\n    except ValueError:\n        return None\n\n\n----\n2026-10-16T06:18:38Z,674d08af,revise,anthropic,Opus 5.5,xhigh,36.0,31,4\r\n2026-10-16T06:22:41Z,674d08af,revise,anthropic,Opus Add a rule only after you have had to correct the same thing\\r\\ntwice.\\r\\n\\r\\n## What this repo is\\r\\n\\r\\nA small web CMS for IPHS 400 Mini-Project #2. A local admin console\\r\\n(FastAPI + Jinja + SQLite) writes content; `cms publish` renders the published\\r\\ncontent into `site/` as static HTML, which is deployed to GitHub Pages. The\\r\\nadmin console never goes on the public internet.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Hard constraints\\r\\n\\r\\n- Published HTML uses **relative** paths only. Never `href=\\\"/...\\\"` or `src=\\\"/...\\\"`.\\r\\n- Never commit `.env`, `*.db`, keys, or tokens. Read secrets from the environment.\\r\\n- Passwords are hashed with argon2. Never store or log a plain password.\\r\\n- Every state-changing form carries a CSRF token.\\r\\n- User-written Markdown is sanitized before it is rendered anywhere.\\r\\n- `site/` is generated. Never edit it by hand.\\r\\n- Only published content reaches `site/`. The user writes them to keep rubric factor A (human authenticity)\\\"\\nmetadata:\\n  node_type: memory\\n  type: feedback\\n  originSessionId: 674d08af-c0fa-4702-8ebe-af8ec4dc7e5e\\n  modified: 2026-10-16T05:03:25.042Z\\n---\\n\\nLeave README.md, the report, `notes/cms-field-notes.md` and any client-vocabulary text in CONTEXT.md to the user entirely: no edits and no suggested wording. Give facts only: line numbers, ledger numbers, SHAs, issue numbers, and what's wrong or missing against the rubric.\\n\\n**Why:** on 2026-10-16 the user said: \\\"Now leave README.md alone. I'll be writing this after all to preserve points on A for human authenticity and judgement, rather than having AI suggestions.\\\" Rubric factor A (17 pts) penalizes unedited AI output in human-facing text.\\n\\n**How to apply:** for human-facing docs, report gaps as evidence plus the rubric item, never as drafted sentences. Code, tests, issue bodies, review-findings comments and handoffs stay mine. See [[mp2-revision]].\\n---\\nname: mp2-repo-reference\\ndescription: \\\"Where MP2 CMS project artifacts live — GitHub repo, issue numbering, environment quirks\\\"\\nmetadata:\\n  node_type: memory\\n  type: reference\\n  originSessionId: 77346485-805e-46b5-86aa-e4a072c5d79e\\n  modified: 2026-10-06T15:22:59.034Z\\n---\\n\\nGitHub repo: `Rajan-Thakur/iphs400-mp2-cms` (public). Spec is issue #1 (kept open permanently, per the to-tickets convention of never closing a parent issue). Tickets T01–T14 are issues #2–#15 respectively, all closed as of 2026-10-06. A real local `.env` (gitignored, `CMS_STUDENT=rajan-thakur`) and `cms.db` now exist; the app loads `.env` itself.\\n\\n**Environment quirks in this working copy** (`C:\\\\Rajan\\\\E disk\\\\My work\\\\IPHS400\\\\code\\\\mp2-cms`, Windows):\\n- A stray user-level `PYTHONPATH` env var shadows the project venv and breaks `pytest`/`uv run` with `ImportError: cannot import name '_console_main' from '_pytest.config'`. Always `export PYTHONPATH=` (Bash) before any `uv run` invocation in this repo.\\n- `uv.exe` is not on PATH in this shell; full path is `C:\\\\Users\\\\rajan\\\\AppData\\\\Local\\\\Microsoft\\\\WinGet\\\\Packages\\\\astral-sh.uv_Microsoft.Winget.Source_8wekyb3d8bbwe\\\\uv.exe`.\\n- `gh.exe` is at `C:\\\\Program Files\\\\GitHub CLI\\\\gh.exe`, authenticated as `Rajan-Thakur`.\\n- `site/` (the static export `render_site()`/`cms publish` writes) is gitignored and untracked — regenerating/overwriting it locally during smoke tests is always safe, never a real deploy.\\n---\\nname: mp2-review-process\\ndescription: The per-ticket implementation and code-review workflow used successfully across all 9 MP2 CMS tickets\\nmetadata:\\n  node_type: memory\\n  type: feedback\\n  originSessionId: 77346485-805e-46b5-86aa-e4a072c5d79e\\n  modified: 2026-10-06T07:41:53.102Z\\n---\\n\\nFor each ticket in the MP2 CMS build, the workflow that ran clean across all 9 tickets without correction was: implement with tests written alongside (not strict TDD, but test-covering every acceptance criterion), run the full suite, smoke-test over real HTTP with a throwaway seeded db (not just `TestClient`) for anything UI/template-related, then launch exactly two parallel background sub-agents — one \\\"Standards axis\\\" (documented repo standards + a fixed Fowler smell baseline) and one \\\"Spec axis\\\" (faithfulness to the ticket's acceptance criteria) — each reading the real diff and relevant context files fresh, each reporting back under 350-450 words. Wait for both before fixing anything, so fixes land in one batch. Post the findings-and-resolutions as a GitHub issue comment on that ticket's issue *before* the closing commit (CLAUDE.md hard-requires this), then commit with `T0N: <title>` ... `Closes #N`, push, confirm auto-close.\\n\\n**Why:** CLAUDE.md requires posting review findings before the closing commit, and the two-axis separation (from the mattpocock-skills `/code-review` skill) stops one axis's pass from masking the other's fail. Running both as backgrounded forks let the main thread stay responsive instead of blocking serially on two long sub-agent calls per ticket.\\n\\n**How to apply:** Real findings get fixed in the code; judgement calls the reviewer itself frames as \\\"reasonable, not a violation\\\" get left alone and explicitly noted as a deliberate non-fix in the closing comment, with the reasoning — never silently dropped, never fixed reflexively either. When a review surfaces something genuinely unrelated to the current ticket (e.g. a missing nav link from an earlier closed ticket, or a shared helper worth extracting across already-committed files), it's fine to fix it in the same commit as long as it's small, safe, and explicitly disclosed in the commit message/review comment as a deliberate aside rather than hidden in an unrelated diff.\\n\\nAlways stage files explicitly (`git add <specific paths>`), never `-A`/`.`, and check `git status` before every stage — this project had the user's own in-progress, uncommitted work (an AI Use Statement draft in README.md) sitting in the tree for several tickets in a row; it was never swept into a commit because staging was always explicit.\\n---\\nname: mp2-revision\\ndescription: \\\"MP2 revision round (Oct 2026): rubric changed upstream, resubmission deadline, and the gap-analysis findings to fix\\\"\\nmetadata:\\n  node_type: memory\\n  type: project\\n  originSessionId: 674d08af-c0fa-4702-8ebe-af8ec4dc7e5e\\n  modified: 2026-10-16T07:42:37.060Z\\n---\\n\\n**Revision deadline:** the revised `mp2-final` plus the Stage 2 email must be in by **Fri 2026-10-16 2:40pm EDT** (no-penalty grace; the due date was Thu 10/15). A revised `mp2-final` *replaces* the earlier grade. After that comes MP2plus (due Tue 10/20 2:40pm, grace Wed 10/21; needs a `mp2plus-base` tag on the commit graded for MP2).\\n\\n**Upstream rubric changes** (`git fetch template` → template/main 0a9eee4; the local docs/ copy is still v1): MP2 is now a numeric score out of 100 with no pass line, and Stage 1's 15 points are credited for the `mp2-mvp` tag. Stretch features **earn nothing in MP2**. MP2plus doesn't score a feature already present at `mp2plus-base`, so T15/T16 (revisions, scheduled publishing) are unscored everywhere. Don't merge template/main: it brings the instructor's ledger rows and sample transcripts.\\n\\n**Gap analysis 2026-10-16** (fresh clone at 2bcabc7: checker 22/22, 264 tests, judge-style walkthrough 59/59), estimated about 92/100. Fixable losses:\\n- G2: README has no Live URL section (the checker passes only on the localhost URL) and no *Backends used* table; starter boilerplate is still there.\\n- F2: gh-pages has `href=\\\"/contact-us.html\\\"` in about.html and posts/hour-of-code.html, so both are 404s. These are root-absolute links from the user's Markdown; the checker only greps main.\\n- B7(c): the report's 25%/65% figures are ctx_pct, not five_h_pct. `usage_report.py` shows grill+spec ≈11% against a 20% plan, which is *under* plan.\\n- G1: the revision-count claims are unverifiable (the db is local), (b) is thin, and a dangling sentence is uncommitted.\\n- A2: the field notes never mention the client, and CONTEXT.md line 3 contradicts itself.\\n- H2 risk: 3 raw `.jsonl` files sit in docs/transcripts. The `.md` transcripts are raw JSONL too.\\n\\nPermanent losses: B3 (T09/T12 are test-only slices) and B6(b) (T01–T14 ran in one session).\\n\\n**Done 2026-10-16:**\\n- **F2 fixed via T17** (issue #20, commit de91174, pushed, issue closed after the findings comment). Body links starting with `/` are rewritten to `./` / `../` inside nh3's attribute_filter. The review caught an XSS in the first version (an empty prefix let `/javascript:` through on Pages); the prefix is now never empty. 286 tests pass, checker 22/22.\\n- Redeployed: gh-pages 8850075; the live crawl is clean.\\n- `prof_response.md` was added to `.git/info/exclude`.\\n- The user added a README Live URL section and a Backends used table. Still wrong in the README: the Oct 20 deadline at line 44, Run locally blocking on `serve`, stale starter text.\\n- The `.md` transcripts being raw JSONL is the template hook's own design (`save_transcript.py`), so that's fine. The 3 `*.jsonl` files were added by hand and still need untracking (H2).\\n\\n**Gap analysis #2, 2026-10-16 ~2:45am** (template/main still 0a9eee4; fresh clone de91174: walk 59/59, live crawl clean, checker 22/22, 286 tests). Estimate **~95/100** (range 91–98), assuming the working tree is committed, the `.jsonl` deletions are committed, `mp2-final` is moved, and the email goes out by 2:40pm.\\n- The user's edits fixed A2 (field notes name the client), B7(c) (6/18/13% match `usage_report.py`), and the README Live URL and deadline.\\n- **B3 is now likely 1, not 2.** T17 is labelled `ticket` and touches only mdrender/publish/tests. That makes 10 of 15 core tickets clear slices (67%). T02 and T07 are borderline.\\n- **G1 is still 2.** Report claims that are wrong or can't be checked: the revision counts (line 7); \\\"all transcripts from one session\\\" (line 16, but there are 5 sessions); the 79% peak was at T13, not extra credit (line 20); \\\"plan 3%/ticket\\\" when the plan says 8+3 (lines 20–21); grill/spec actually came in under plan; T10 has no ledger rows. (I also flagged e9dfb18 as wrongly listed; that was a mistake. The 14:40Z review flagged spend(), so e9dfb18 is a real fix from that review.)\\n- **G2 risks:** the \\\"Run it\\\" section at lines 9–15 skips seed_demo; `cms deploy` needs push rights; starter boilerplate is still in; line 87 omits xHigh.\\n- **Test-env trap:** a leftover server from session 674d08af still listens on :8765. Windows lets two servers share the port, so the walk failed C6 falsely. Use the copy at port 8791 in session 1055f71e's scratchpad `walk/walk.py`.\\n\\n**Gap analysis #3, 3:25am:** still ~95, checker 22/22.\\n- **Fixed by the user:** README title, Start here, line 87; report lines 16 and 20 (now 54%); CONTEXT.md.\\n- **New issues:**\\n  - Report line 21 now claims /code-review ran only after all tickets. The transcript shows per-ticket two-axis reviews for T01–T09 (Oct 6, 05:14–07:39Z), and the comments on #2–#10 confirm it.\\n  - README \\\"Run it\\\" (lines 5–10) runs seed before `cp .env.example`, so seed exits 1 and no users exist.\\n- The 18:47 grill duplicate came from the user re-pasting after their laptop slept; it isn't an authenticity issue.\\n- The user proposed committing cms.db to back up the revision counts. The usage ledger labels every turn with it, so set it before you start. Process and security exercises set their id instead, for example `echo S2 > .claude/state/phase`.\n140:| Your exercise has no row in `usage_report.py` | the phase was set after the session started | set it first next time; the ledger cannot be relabelled |\n585:1. Before your next exercise, in your terminal and not inside Claude (so the ledger does not change between the run and the commit): `uv run python scripts/usage_report.py --remaining 3`, where 3 stands for the number of exercises still in your plan. **Expected:** the phase table, then `Tickets done: ...` and `Forecast: 3 tickets need ~X% of the weekly cap; Y% remains → FITS` (or `TIGHT`, or `OVER BUDGET`).\n586:2. Paste those two lines into `notes/mp2plus-plan.md` under `## P4 before`, name the exercise you do next, and commit the plan with `notes/usage-ledger.csv` (message `P4: before`). Push. If the verdict is not `FITS`, apply MP2's fixes in order (MP2 manual Part 5.6) or pick a smaller exercise.\n588:4. In your report, under `## P4`: paste the two lines, a cost table (one row per MP2plus phase, copied from the phase table: phase, turns, 5h spent, weekly), and two or three sentences on the largest gap between forecast and actual. Commit the report with `notes/usage-ledger.csv` (message `P4: after`) and push.\n595:| 1 | The \"before\" lines (`Tickets done` and `Forecast`) are in `notes/mp2plus-plan.md`, committed together with `notes/usage-ledger.csv` before the first commit of the exercise the plan names next | read | 1 |\n596:| 2 | The \"after\" lines are in the report, committed together with `notes/usage-ledger.csv` after at least one other exercise is done | read | 1 |\n----\n### 1.2 Process (P1–P4)\n\n#### P1 Pull-request workflow (6 pts, about 30–45 min)\n- **Goal.** Ship one change through a branch, a pull request and a review before the merge, the way most teams work.\n- **Prerequisites.** Part 0 complete. P1 carries one change: a feature from this menu (scored as that feature too), or one item from your MP2 feedback's repair list, written as a small issue. A repair earns P1's points only, not MP2 points.\n- **Roles.** No new routes of its own; a carried feature keeps its own Roles line.\n\n**Steps.**\n1. Create the issue first. For a feature, that feature's step 1. For a repair: `gh issue create --label stretch --title \"MP2plus P1: <the repair>\"`, body = two to four acceptance steps, then `Blocked by: none`. Set the phase: `echo T<issue#> > .claude/state/phase` for a feature, `echo P1 > .claude/state/phase` for a repair.\n2. Branch: `git switch main && git pull && git switch -c mp2plus-<slug>`.\n3. `/clear`, then the feature's prompt or the prompt below; let it commit on the branch.\n4. `git push -u origin mp2plus-<slug>`, then `gh pr create --title \"MP2plus P1: <the change>\" --body \"Closes #<issue#>\"`.\n5. `/code-review`, then: `Post the code-review findings and how each was resolved as a comment on pull request #<pr#>, then commit and push the fixes to this branch.`\n6. `gh pr merge <pr#> --merge --delete-branch`, then `git switch main && git pull` and `gh issue view <issue#>`. **Expected:** CLOSED, closed by your pull request, with the review comment on the PR dated before the merge.","is_error":false}]},"uuid":"21f420cf-c5e2-4d14-a9d5-34458093d33a","timestamp":"2026-10-21T01:22:54.192Z","toolUseResult":{"stdout":"61:2026-10-16T04:13:14Z,674d08af,revise\n62:2026-10-16T04:23:42Z,674d08af,T17\n63:2026-10-16T04:31:12Z,674d08af,T17\n64:2026-10-16T04:41:24Z,674d08af,revise\n65:2026-10-16T05:03:55Z,674d08af,revise\n66:2026-10-16T06:06:49Z,674d08af,revise\n67:2026-10-16T06:16:10Z,674d08af,revise\n68:2026-10-16T06:17:23Z,674d08af,revise\n69:2026-10-16T06:18:38Z,674d08af,revise\n70:2026-10-16T06:22:41Z,674d08af,revise\n----\n51:- **Your MP2 setup still working:** `uv`, `gh` logged in, Claude Code with the mattpocock skills (`/implement`, `/tdd`, `/code-review`, `/prototype`, `/handoff`), the hooks, and the usage ledger.\n123:2. **The phase, before the session.** `echo T14 > .claude/state/phase`, with your issue number for 14. {"parentUuid":"9269dd2d-1fe4-4009-b003-45c822411de1","isSidechain":false,"message":{"model":"claude-opus-5-5","id":"msg_011CgEWPGbjmoSu6YmmqJYHh","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"text","text":"The phase is a label that gets stamped on every Claude turn. \"Before the session\" means you write that label after you've created the exercise's issue and before you start building it with `/clear` and the prompt. If you set it later, the turns that already happened keep the old label.\n\n**How it works here**\n- `.claude/state/phase` holds a single word, which right now is `revise`.\n- The Stop hook in `.claude/hooks/ctx_guard.py:79` (`log_usage`) reads that file at the end of every Claude turn. It writes the word into the `phase` column of the row it adds to `notes/usage-ledger.csv`.\n- `scripts/usage_report.py` groups the ledger by that column. It only counts labels of the form `T<digits>` as tickets (`ticket_phases`, line 74).\n- Rows that are already written never change. That's what the manual means by \"the ledger cannot be relabelled.\"\n\nYour own ledger shows this. In session `674d08af` on Oct 16 (the T17 work), rows 61–70 read `revise, T17, T17, revise, revise…`. The label changed partway through, so only two turns were counted as T17. The manual's \"no row\" symptom is the extreme case, where the phase is set after all the work is done or never set at all.\n\n**What \"the session\" is**\nIt's the Claude conversation where you build the exercise, which runs from runbook step 3 (`/clear` plus the prompt) onward. The phase can't be set before step 1, because the label is the issue number and you don't have it until `gh issue create` prints it. So it goes between steps 1 and 3.\n\n**The label for each exercise in your plan**\n| Exercise | Phase |\n|---|---|\n| F1 | `T<F1's issue #>` |\n| F5 | `T<F5's issue #>` |\n| F8 (carried by P1) | `T<F8's issue #>`. P1 step 1 says a carried feature uses the feature's number, so there's no separate `P1` label |\n| S1 | `S1` |\n\nDon't use `F1`: features are labelled with the issue number, not the menu id. Issues and PRs share one numbering sequence, so use whatever number `gh` prints rather than guessing.\n\n**Windows catch**\nThe manual's `echo T14 > …` assumes bash. I tested what happens in this machine's PowerShell 5.1:\n- **`echo T21 > file`:** the hook reads it back as `'ï»¿T21'` (an invisible marker at the start of the file breaks the label), and `usage_report.py` doesn't count that as a ticket.\n- **`Set-Content .claude\\state\\phase T21`:** it reads back as `'T21'`, which is correct. In Git Bash, plain `echo T21 > .claude/state/phase` also works.\n\nHandoff 03 (`docs/handoff/03_t16-scheduled-publishing_20261007.md:25`) recorded the same trap.\n\n**For F1, the order is:**\n1. In your terminal: `gh issue create --label stretch --title \"MP2plus F1: Media uploads with alt text\"`, and note the number.\n2. `Set-Content .claude\\state\\phase T<that number>`\n3. In Claude: `/clear`, then the F1 prompt with that issue number.\n\n**Does it affect your score?** In the MP2plus rubric, only P4's rows read the phase (rubric lines 259–260). P4 isn't in your plan, so a wrong label won't directly cost points on F1, F5, F8, P1 or S1. It still matters because the ledger is your record of what each exercise cost, and `CLAUDE.md` requires the label.
